| DEC-28 | Hashing performance (CS1 review M-1): on agentbox (no SHA-NI), `bitcoin_hashes` software SHA-256 runs ~51.7 instr/byte vs Core's SSE4 transform at ~36.8, making script validation ~14% slower than dashd per input. Decisions: (a) new task **SH1**: a Core-class SHA-256 backend (SSE4 single-stream, AVX2 8-way and SHA-NI where present, the multi-way double-SHA256 for 64-byte inputs used by merkle), runtime-dispatched, bit-exact (test vectors + differential against Core's implementations); used by every node crate through CS0's single hashing module. (b) CS1's sighash prefix cache (C3) and per-opcode multisig memo (C2) are REQUIRED before CS1's benchmark gate, not optional; their state gets API slots now. (c) Add Core-side comparison benches: sighash, one real block's inputs, a worst-case quadratic-hashing block. (d) 2x on per-input script validation at equal threads is not reachable (ceiling ~1.47x); record it as a declared shortfall in A1 §3 unless parallelism changes the picture. | Never-slower rule | CS1 review |

Built **with or from dash-spv**, not beside it; rdashd gains an SPV mode. Rule **R2 (A1 §1.1: node crates never depend on the dashcore/dash-spv stack) is suspended** and will be rewritten by the shared-base design. Mobile constraints bind the shared base: iOS/Android FFI, binary size, memory, storage engine. The dash-spv maintainers' refactor (ZocoLini #902) works in the same code: coordinate, don't fork. CV5 is no longer optional post-M7; the design picks its point. Design: **SB0**, a Fable ∥ Opus reconciled design (fits DEC-88's top tier). Until SB0 is approved: no new P2-, CH1, EV/LQ implementation starts; work below or beside the shared boundary continues where SB0 cannot change it (see STATUS). | Two parallel stacks double the network, sync and LLMQ surface and split mobile and node fixes. | pasta direction item 1 |

| DEC-95 | **CH2 coins memory follows Core's semantics, not a hard per-op ceiling** (manager, 2026-10-09; supersedes DEC-80 Q4 rule O and DEC-94 Q1; CH2 r3 Sol G-R3-M1–M3). Core (v24 `validation.cpp:2945–2966, 3036–3046`) lets the coins cache grow during a block and checks it only in `FlushStateToDisk` after the block: CRITICAL when usage > `nTotalSpace` (full flush), LARGE at max(90 %, `nTotalSpace − MAX_BLOCK_COINSDB_USAGE_BYTES`) for the periodic flush. CH2 does the same: budget B = `nTotalSpace`, accounted exactly (tagging allocator in tests), **checked at Core's points** (after each block / at Core's flush modes), with Core's LARGE headroom; **no per-op reservation, streaming admission or abort-and-retry**. Mid-block growth is allowed; the peak is bounded by consensus block limits and is gated as evidence, not as an invariant: CH2c/DEC-43 compare `rss_anon_peak` and peak coins usage against dashd's (never higher). Consequences: G-R3-M1/M2 are moot; G-R3-M3 becomes "every layer layout must grow past its presize (rehash or overflow) so a valid block always completes", tested with the `-dbcache=4` 50,000-output and long-input witnesses. Freezing never happens mid-block (unchanged). | Three review rounds could not make the stricter-than-Core invariant implementable; Core's own semantics are simpler and are the parity target. | CH2 r3 Sol |

Gates: native peak ≤ B and RSS/peak never above dashd's (DEC-43/96). It equals Core's answer whenever no flush separates the connect from the query (the deterministic case `mempool_accept.py` and peers exercise). Across differing flush points the `txn-already-known` / `bad-txns-inputs-missingorspent` reason and the orphan-parent request may differ from Core's; this is a **declared allowed difference** (compat ALLOWED-DIFFS; not consensus; DEC-83 parity is conceptual). | DEC-104 | **SB0 r1 rulings** (Sol r1 REWORK, reviews/SB0-design-r1-sol.md). Fix as Sol proposes: **M1** the shared v24 contextual-header check uses mockable local `NodeClock`, never adjusted time; **M2** the node push is unconditional, returning pause state (Core `PushMessage` + `fPauseSend`, P2a retained-memory accounting); any hard admission budget lives in the SPV processor only; **M6** the SQLite snapshot pins its read view before returning; **M9** reconcile table/prose edges (CS3→SB4, LQ1b→SB11, SB15→SB18, SB15/16→SB17 final cells) and rerun `cpm.py` before recording M2 impact. Simplifying rulings (manager, to match Core semantics and cut scope): **M5** there is **no SPV→full header import**: switching rdashd `-spv` to full starts a normal Core-style header sync from genesis over P2P (header sync is cheap); the SPV store is never opened by full mode, so P10 holds trivially; SB18 loses the import. **M3** the SPV anchor is the newest checkpoint whose forward history covers the MN pipeline's maximum historical lookback (largest DIP24 rotation lookback + DKG/mining window, computed in the design); if the newest is too close to the tip, use the previous one (forward header sync of ≤ 50,000 headers is cheap). No authenticated backfill below the anchor. **M4** the cold store keeps every accepted header as a full record keyed by hash with a stable ID and branch status; the active height index is a projection; anchored clients get an explicit partial-ancestry interface (floor = anchor − 24), the node's complete-ancestry `ChainAncestry` contract is unchanged. **M8** SPV trusts only commitment-verified quorum public keys (commitment hash + quorumSig + membersSig as Core verifies them) for ISLock/ChainLock/recovered-sig checks; SPV makes **no claim of exact reconstructed membership**; exact membership (DIP24 member lists) is node-only, with `mnxcheck` as the shadow comparison. SB8's gate tests what SPV actually uses. **M7** #902's facade is **extended, not kept verbatim**: bounded subscription handles, cancel, terminal `RequestFailed`, logical-request vs peer-attempt ids, with a documented migration for its consumers and tests; coordination with #902's author goes through pasta's Q1. Minors m1–m5 are fixed as proposed (m4: `dash-pow` on the opt-level 3 list until the backend switch; mobile size and speed claims labelled pending measurement). | Fix Core-exactness errors; replace two unbounded features (SPV→full import, backfill) and one unprovable claim (exact SPV membership) with Core-style simpler behaviour. | SB0 r1 Sol | SB0, SB1–SB19, EV0, LQ1, P2a |
| DEC-105 | **SB0 r2 rulings** (Sol r2 REWORK, reviews/SB0-design-r2-sol.md; DEC-104 simplifications stay). Adopt Sol's fixes, which all match Core semantics: **F1** the MN pipeline's startup confirmation covers the *actual* required history: the bases, rotation work blocks and diff targets of the commitments that are really active (Core counts mined commitments, not elapsed intervals, so failed DKG rounds keep old quorums active); if any lies below the anchor, re-anchor to an earlier checkpoint, ultimately genesis; L_max stays only as a regular-DKG sizing estimate, never as a bound; still no backfill. **F2** the 25-header pack is for post-DGW checkpoint anchors only; a genesis-origin chain (including the windowed configuration) reads pre-DGW history through a fallible context that holds the selected rule's real lookback (BTC 576 = Dash retarget interval 86,400/150 / KGW up to 4,032; corrected from 2,016 per SB0 rev2) before calling the unchanged pure functions; the node's complete-ancestry contract is unchanged. **F3** a retained snapshot resolves below-window heights by ancestry from *its own* tip id over the immutable branch records; the live `active_at` projection serves live queries only (CH2 F8 coherence). **F4** SPV selects the Core-defined quorum from the authenticated canonical scan first (order, count and the selection hash/height rules, independent of local verification), then requires that exact commitment to be `Verified`, otherwise unavailable; it never re-selects over a filtered set. **f1** the signer-mutation claim is limited to a changed signing-key multiset, with a permutation case whose verdict is unchanged; **f2** CPM text labels coding float and merge slack separately (SB4/SB5 1.5, CH1-node 1.5, SB6/P2a-node 0). | Each finding is a Core-semantics or coherence gap, not a stricter-than-Core demand. | SB0 r2 Sol | SB0, SB4, SB5, SB8, SB11 |
| DEC-106 | **E0-09 r4 rulings** (desktop; Sol r4, reviews/DW-E0-09-r4-sol.md). **#1 fix:** an unlock or SPV-setup task panic goes through the same panic-aware probe/abandon boundary as command failures, and the typed `TASK_PANICKED` survives the `--spv` bridge. **#2 accepted limit:** if the caller keeps stdout open but stops reading it, dwcli blocks on the write like any POSIX CLI. Callers must drain stdout. This goes in the m4 contract's dwcli section. The only required change is to zeroize the passphrase *before* the terminal envelope writes, so a blocked write never holds an unwiped secret. No non-blocking stdout, writer thread or timeout. | Stopping at standard CLI semantics; the security property (secret wiped) holds without bounding writes. | DW-E0-09 r4 | E0-09 |
| DEC-107 | **SB0 r3 rulings** (Sol r3 REWORK, reviews/SB0-design-r3-sol.md; manager simplification per the CH2 lesson). **M1 fix:** RuleContext loads KGW's `PastBlocksMax + 1` entries, including the loop-exit predecessor and clipped at genesis; the pure functions stay unchanged. **M2/M3/m2 removed by ruling: no runtime re-anchor or rebuild.** The anchor is chosen only during *initial* sync, before the MN pipeline, wallet subscribers or locks hold any state. Sync headers from a candidate checkpoint, run DEC-105's coverage check against the authenticated active set, and fall back to an earlier checkpoint, down to genesis, while the store holds headers only. Once established, the anchor never moves. A later coverage miss marks the affected quorums `Unavailable` (liveness loss only) and surfaces an operator-visible diagnostic. Re-anchoring an established wallet is a user-initiated resync, outside SB0. This removes the locator/state rebuild machine and any path that could drop a verified ChainLock. **M4 fix, bounded:** define a finite recent-lock horizon from local chain progress: the client retains or fetches the authenticated canonical active set for signed heights within the last H_keep blocks. H_keep covers the largest CL/IS quorum type's DKG interval plus SIGN_HEIGHT_OFFSET plus a relay-delay margin, computed per network in the design. Locks needing older sets report unavailable. Select once, then require Verified (DEC-105 F4). **m1:** fix the CH1-node float/date contradiction. | Three of four r3 majors came from the new re-anchor machinery; dropping runtime re-anchor is simpler and keeps safety. | SB0 r3 Sol | SB0, SB4, SB5, SB11, SB15 |

| DEC-110 | **E0-09: end the panic-path rounds with one general mechanism** (Sol r5, reviews/DW-E0-09-r5-sol.md). Four Sol rounds found one more panic path each time into a wedged `engine.shutdown()`: command, unlock, SPV start, SPV stop. Two mechanisms replace path-by-path handling. (1) **An engine-wide poison flag:** dw-engine's task wrapper sets a process-wide `engine_poisoned` flag whenever any engine task panics, however its result is later mapped (String errors, timeouts or warnings included). dwcli checks the flag before any SPV stop or shutdown, and a set flag means probe-then-abandon. (2) **Bounded shutdown:** every dwcli shutdown, healthy or not, runs under a deadline (10 s, configurable). If it misses the deadline, dwcli wipes secrets, writes the envelopes best-effort and exits 1 (the DEC-106 path). Together these cover any undiscovered path. Tests: the two r5 paths plus one generic wedged-shutdown test that has no panic at all. | Stop the whack-a-mole; a deadline is the only property that holds for paths nobody has found yet. | DW-E0-09 r5 | E0-09 |

It is bound to the process-wide cache owner, so cache state survives failed and proposal attempts. `SnapshotRows::store` updates the overlay and records the ordered effect, and later reads see the last overwrite, matching Core's unconditional raw write (`snapshot.cpp:320`). `EffectSink` stays write-only, and effects apply even on failure or `fJustCheck`, as Core's do. LQ1a codes against this contract. **M1, M2 and M3:** fix as Sol proposes (orphan-rule-safe impl site; error propagation on ancestry-reading predicates and iterators; mnxcheck compares on the same cached-membership semantics as the node, or reports only Core-defined equal cases). **M4:** recompute CPM with the true EV0 edges. These are reversible build/config choices. **Q1, Q2 and Q6 stay with pasta:** Q2 (pre-clear SQLite and the shared-crate shape with maintainers) is needed by about week 9–10, before SB10/SB12. While an attempt is open, every external members, materializer, predictor and snapshot-read route, and mnxcheck's miss path, waits until CH7a has applied that attempt's effect log, on every outcome, and released the attempt. Nobody outside the attempt ever sees its published members together with its unapplied rows, the state Core cannot expose (snapshot.cpp:326 writes the row before utils.cpp:710 publishes). We don't add a shared pending-write view. | DEC-114 | **E0-06: catch-up silence uses authoritative times only** (Sol r1 M2, reviews/DW-E0-06-r1-sol.md; simplify-early rule). (1) An event's age comes only from authoritative times: the block time of the confirmed Core tx for payments, and Platform `$createdAt` or the marketplace row time for names and requests. Local fetch or observation time is never an age. (2) Every recovery phase runs under `catch_up(id)`: the bring-up, DP1-05's names pass and `discover_identities`, and the bring-up queued after discovery. While the guard is held, everything is stored read. (3) Outside the guard, an event older than the catch-up boundary by authoritative time is stored read. An unconfirmed payment, or one with no authoritative time, is news. (4) Declared residual: an old event that first surfaces outside any guard and has no authoritative time may show as unread once. This is cosmetic: no trust flag or money movement depends on it. | 2026-10-09 |

An SPV header is eligible for `fork_target` only if (a) no ancestor is failed or `BLOCK_CONFLICT_CHAINLOCK`, the same walk as Core's `FindMostWorkChain`, and (b) it descends from the current `cl_floor` block when one exists; (b) also covers the case where the lock is already active and Core marks nothing. Pick the most-work eligible header. Every published SPV tip must respect the floor. **R6-M2:** if the wallet checkpoint hash is unknown to the SPV store, never infer a fork point from heights. Do one `truncate_above(H)` and rescan, where H is the durable ChainLock floor height capped at min(wallet height, durable SPV tip), or else the anchor height. A durable lock below the wallet's height can't have been crossed by a published branch (R6-M1), so H is on the retained chain. The cost is a rare post-power-loss rescan. Don't reacquire lost branch ancestry. **R6-M3:** a hash lookup at h after pruning fetches authenticated null-base diffs at two targets, h−W_P and h (deduplicated, with the existing timeouts). Cached rows and fetched results use the same authorization predicate. This needs every quorum to stay active for more than W_P blocks, i.e. interval × active count > W_P for every LLMQ type, in every supported network config. A params test asserts this, and config load refuses a config that breaks it. A quorum therefore can't be mined and replaced between the two targets. **R6-m1:** quote run-specific milestone dates (A/B/stress 18.5/30.0/41.5; C 21.5/31.0/42.5). | 2026-10-09 |

The params test asserts the minimum-lifetime form: interval × cycles active (1 for rotated types) − mining-window width > W_P. Config load refuses a config only when a served type breaks it. Inside a V command, nested calls reuse its `ExternalToken` inline. If the OS can't create the teardown thread or its fallback, that counts as a missed deadline. The engine is abandoned at once through the DEC-106 path: wipe the passphrase, write the owed lines best effort, exit 1. On that path the runtime and the engine are **never dropped on the caller's thread**: use `mem::forget`, or let `process::exit` reclaim them. | DEC-135 | **E0-06 mechanisms** (Sol r3; the third round on provenance and boundary, so mechanisms rather than patches). (1) **Durable latch before use:** the trust fallback may be used only after `trust.fallback_used_at` is durably written. If the write fails, the fallback is not used for that fetch and the fetch fails or degrades as if no fallback existed. The in-memory latch is only a cache of the durable one and never replaces it. An unreadable latch still counts as set. (2) **Atomic monotone boundary:** the catch-up boundary moves only through one SQL upsert computing `max(stored, new)`, and the cache takes the value that statement stored (`RETURNING`), so no interleaving can lower it. (3) **Advance where the phase starts:** the boundary advance lives in the shared bring-up path for a wallet with an identity on file, not in the supervisor's `Readmit` arm, so every route that starts that phase (supervisor, replacement bring-up after an SPV restart, discovery) advances. | 2026-10-09 |

The wallet stores a **block locator** (Core's `CBlockLocator` shape, as `CWallet` keeps its best-block locator) with each committed sync record. On reopen with an unknown hash, it rewinds to the first locator entry present on the restored chain, the true fork point, falling back to the anchor. The floor is not used as a recovery bound. This supersedes DEC-115's unknown-hash rule. **R4-M2:** node `enforce_block` holds the tip mutex for the whole composite (all staged disconnects, the final snapshot store and the event handoff), as Core holds `cs_main` through `EnforceBlock`. | DEC-143 | **E0-06 r4: two ordering rules, then out of the Sol loop.** (1) **Durability is established, never inferred:** `latch_durable` becomes true only after a successful `checkpoint_full()` in this process. `Provenance::open()` with an existing latch row sets `fallback_used = true`, and a fallback use then checkpoints before proceeding; until a checkpoint succeeds, the fallback is refused. (2) **Consume after use:** the recovery marker is taken only after the bring-up has completed the boundary advance it guards. A cancel before the advance leaves the marker for the next bring-up, and a cancel after it is harmless because the advance is monotone and durable. Core reaches the same end by revalidating loaded transactions against the active chain (`CWallet::LoadToWallet`); a single transaction is the simpler equivalent for SPV. **R5-M2:** D42 is withdrawn. The CH2 `IF_NEEDED` pressure check, and its CRITICAL wait, runs after **every** disconnect inside the `enforce_block` hold, as Core's `FlushStateToDisk(IF_NEEDED)` runs in each `DisconnectTip` under `cs_main`. The deadlock is removed at its source by a **lock-order contract for CH2b**: W, the flush writer, never acquires the tip mutex, the feed/hub lock, or any lock V may hold during enforcement on its path from freeze to release. W re-checks `closing` before each wait and treats a closing target as satisfied. | DEC-147 | **SB4 harness vectors are state oracles, with idempotent enforcement repeats allowed** (sb4-core-scenarios Sol r1 R1-M1). In dashd the number of ChainLock enforcement passes after a lock on a header-only branch depends on thread timing; it has no fixed bound. So a vector asserts the semantic state (statuses, tip, `best_header`, floor, records) and the trace up to and including the first enforcement boundary of each lock, the ABC disconnect/connect order included. Further enforcement passes are allowed repeats: zero or more, each idempotent, each leaving the asserted state unchanged. `ScriptedV` and SB4's tests compare under that rule, not against an exact hook count. The harness delivers Core's real callbacks under a controlled, recorded schedule instead of dropping them by emission-time prediction, and the vector's provenance names the delivery policy. A schedule that changes semantic state would be a finding against the rule, recorded as a set of allowed states. Same spirit as DEC-95/DEC-138: match Core's semantics, not one sample of its timing. | 2026-10-09 |
| DEC-148 | **ST3a: R7a exemptions follow byte provenance; mixed blocks may be unreadable** (ST3a CP3.1 Sol, CP3.1-1 and CP3.1-2; the second R7a-masking finding, so a mechanism is ruled rather than another narrowing). (1) R7a exempts an interrupted/uninterrupted divergence only when a recovery read returned bytes whose provenance is an inherited pending write. Provenance is tracked per byte through overwrites: a complete overwrite drops the inherited dependency, a partial one keeps it for the bytes it preserves. Historical versions stay selectable for crash outcomes but never exempt by themselves. (2) The model includes the native ext4/XFS outcome Sol demonstrated: a 4 KiB block holding durable bytes plus an unsynced tail can return EIO to a buffered read. The durable bytes stay preserved, never corrupted, but may be unavailable. The required engine behaviour is fail-stop (open or read returns an error, no silent loss, no wrong data). D1 counts "unavailable and the engine fail-stopped" as acceptable, and any successful read must return the durable bytes. No extra filesystem assumption is granted, so A-PSOW keeps its current wording. | 2026-10-09 |

| DEC-152 | **SB5: the producer may block only on O(1) leaf mutexes, as Core's signal enqueue does** (SB5 design Sol r1 R1-M2). The scheduler thread holds that mutex only for O(1) pops. SB5 adopts the same contract and amends SB0 §6.3 P2 to say: "`change_tip` never blocks except on leaf mutexes whose every critical section is O(1)." (1) Every critical section under a subscriber slot mutex is O(1) in Q. A reader takes queued events by swapping the queue out against a spare buffer it owns, never by refilling Q items under the lock, and buffers are reserved up front. (2) Wakers are notification-only, enforced by type: `std::task::Waker::wake_by_ref` or a condvar or eventfd notify. Arbitrary caller closures, I/O, response waits and inline handlers are not allowed. (3) With (1) and (2), the wait is bounded by one O(1) critical section plus scheduling, as in Core. No `try_lock` pending-target machinery is needed. Loom models must include a reader preempted inside the slot critical section. | 2026-10-09 |
| DEC-153 | **Two notification paths: SB5's tip feed and a separate Core-style validation-signal queue** (CH1-node design Sol r1 R1-M6). (1) SB5's F1 stream stays exactly `TipStep` with its sequence and compression semantics. V services its pending fanout between every node tip step inside the hold (SB5 §3.3, DEC-152), so a slow subscriber is handled per step, never deferred to an outer loop. (2) Core's `CValidationInterface` callbacks (`BlockConnected`, `BlockDisconnected`, `UpdatedBlockTip`, `ChainStateFlushed`, …) go to a **separate dispatcher** owned by the node runtime (CH1-node defines it). It is a FIFO callback queue run on its own thread, like `SingleThreadedSchedulerClient`. Enqueueing is O(1) and non-blocking under the hold, per DEC-152. Backpressure applies only at Core's `LimitValidationInterfaceQueue` sites (`validation.cpp:3716`, called at `:3760` and `:3940`, outside `cs_main`), and `SyncWithValidationInterfaceQueue` gets the same semantics. Neither path carries the other's events. | 2026-10-09 |
| DEC-154 | **E0-04 dispatch journal: step markers are append-only; resolution is a tombstone row, never a delete** (DW E0-04.P2a Sol r2 R2-F1; the second marker-durability finding, so a mechanism is ruled rather than another delete/restore patch). (1) A resolution (NotSent, Sent or Ambiguous) is a new row written in J's transaction. Marker rows are never deleted while the artifact or its flow is live. (2) A later observation (for example `Sent` overtaking a `NotSent` resolution) appends a superseding row. Recovery takes the artifact's latest row in sequence and treats any `Sent` or `MaybeSent` row as final evidence that can't be revoked. Nothing has to be "restored", so no window opens between delete and restore. (3) A failed or panicking journal write is treated as possibly committed: the in-memory state stays at the stronger obligation (`Ambiguous` or still-owed) until a later write succeeds, and copies wait on it. (4) Compaction happens only where nothing can be on the wire: wallet removal after its barrier, or a startup sweep of artifacts whose flows are terminal. Same spirit as DEC-138: make the bad state unrepresentable instead of guarding it. | 2026-10-09 |
| DEC-155 | **SB5 memory bounds are enforced by construction, not proven by arithmetic** (SB5 design Sol r2 R2-M1/M2; the second bound-accounting finding after R1-M3). (1) **Controls:** each subscriber has one outstanding flag per control kind, in the slot. The flag is set when V queues that kind and cleared only when the reader *consumes* that message from `next()`, not when it takes it into private staging. While the flag is set, V coalesces into the existing message. Slot messages plus reader-owned messages are therefore at most the number of kinds, by construction. (2) **SPV step peak:** every allocation on the header-step path (TipChange vectors, staging, pending entries, reorg connect legs) is charged to a counted byte budget per step and per feed, with a hard cap derived from the configuration. Exceeding it takes the existing `Publish::Blocked` or backpressure path, never an unbounded allocation. Tests assert the observed maximum is at or below the cap, including directed cases: successive mirror pairs between reader intakes, and the concurrent no-A9 reorg. The design's peak table becomes the cap's derivation plus the observed maxima, not a proof obligation. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-156 | **CH1-node: anything V waits on never waits on V; notifications follow publication** (CH1-node Opus high OH-M1/OH-M2; the second deadlock-class finding after Sol r2 R2-M1, so a mechanism is ruled instead of another listed case). (1) **No waiting on V from what V waits on.** Every task V can wait on (F1 subscriber pop tasks, `ValidationSignals` listeners, W) may post commands to V or to any actor, but may never await a reply that needs V's service, directly or transitively through another actor. This matches Core, where callbacks run without `cs_main` and must not drive ABC synchronously (`v:3738`). It is enforced at runtime: those tasks carry a task-local marker, and `ChainHandle` and the actor reply futures refuse an await made from a marked task in every build (the R2-M1 check generalised). LO-8 becomes a special case of this new rule, LO-9. Model negative controls cover both OH-M2 cycles. (2) **Publish before notify.** Signals produced inside a hold are staged and spliced into the `ValidationSignals` queue in O(1) at `release_tip`, after the snapshot store. UI tip notifications and `TipWatch` fire after publication. A reader woken by a notification therefore always sees the announced tip, as Core's `cs_main` guarantees (`np:6338`, `np:6425`; `waitforblockheight`→`getblockcount`). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-157 | **LO-9 applies to SPV and to feed registration; SB5's budget covers the SPV overlay** (SB5 Opus high M1–M3). It gets the same runtime guard (task-local marker), and SB14 adopts it as ask SB5-Y2. (2) **Registration never blocks on a hold.** `register` and `subscribe_at` return `HoldActive` together with a release wake instead of waiting on the tip mutex. `deregister` step 4 uses `try_lock` and defers the rest to lazy cleanup, so no caller, including CH2b under its hub lock, can wait on a hold that waits on it. (3) **The SPV overlay counts against the budget.** SB4's SPV overlay becomes status-only (a new SB4 ask). A5's invalidation list is bounded and clears the whole cache when it overflows. The mark count is an explicit adversarial term in DEC-155's cap derivation. Budget overruns are loud internal errors, never silent waits, and caps are computed from `size_of`. (4) **Ask labels** are prefixed by the asking design: `SB5-A#`, `CH1-A#`, `SB5-Y#`, and so on. | 2026-10-10 |

| DEC-160 | **E0-04 wallet removal compacts with the startup sweep's predicate, evaluated inside the delete** (DW E0-04.P2a Sol r3 R3-F1; the third marker-durability finding after r1 H1 and r2 R2-F1, so the mechanism is narrowed rather than another in-memory eligibility check added). (1) There is one compaction predicate: an artifact's `step_log` rows may be deleted only if it has no standing marker and no `Sent` or `MaybeSent` row. (2) The wiping `remove_wallet` (§6.5) applies it as a SQL predicate inside the same SQLite transaction as the `DELETE`, not as an in-memory check before an asynchronous delete. A copy's marker append is then serialized with the delete: either the marker lands first and the artifact is kept, or the delete runs first and the marker is a new row written before transport. (3) Whatever the predicate keeps survives removal. That is evidence of something possibly on the wire, which is visible on chain anyway, so keeping it costs no privacy; the residue is bounded by the wallet's possibly-sent artifacts. (4) In memory, removal forgets only the artifacts the delete actually erased. Possibly-out row-less state, standing steps and evidence are kept for the rest. The `dispatch` table keeps its existing entry check. Same spirit as DEC-138 and DEC-154: make the unsafe delete inexpressible instead of guarding it. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-161 | **ST3a I39: a sanctioned fail-stop after an inner-crash EIO is an acceptable R7a outcome** (ST3a CP3.2, I39; applies DEC-148 (2)). Under `Extended` with nested crashes, an inner power loss can tear the recovery's own unsynced write so that the next recovery reads an unreadable block. If that recovery stops with an outcome the design sanctions (Core's read-error abort such as "Failed to read block", or the engine's sanctioned ABORT), with no wrong data used and no silent loss, R7a counts it as a sanctioned fail-stop: allowed, and reported in its own count. This is what Core does: it aborts the same way and needs `-reindex`. These remain R7a failures: any other non-completion, an unsanctioned message, a fail-stop whose unreadable block holds no byte from a torn unsynced write, and any successful read that returns non-durable or wrong bytes. Once this classification lands, the nested `Extended` searches join the nightly. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-162 | **ST3a R7a: exemptions are witnessed by a reference run, never inferred from provenance** (ST3a CP3.2 Sol, CP3.2-1/2/3; the third R7a-masking finding after CP3-3 and CP3.1-1, plus nine internal rounds on the same judgement, so the inference itself is removed rather than narrowed again). (1) **Witness rule.** A nested branch whose interrupted projection differs from the uninterrupted projection passes R7a only if one of two concrete references, run **uninterrupted** under the **same complete input assignment** as the branch, reproduces its projection exactly. The assignment fixes every cell of every crash (inner, outer, earlier) to one value. Cells the branch left undecided are enumerated jointly, with the branch and the reference sharing each value. The two references: (a′) the uninterrupted recovery from the outer image, with the inner crash's choices applied afterwards to the inherited cells still pending; (b) the uninterrupted recovery from the outer image crashed as the inner kind before the recovery's first event, with its `^` cells matched (I36's existing reference). Provenance, the `observed` flags and the size floors may only **select or prune** which references and assignments to try. They never grant a pass. This supersedes the exemption half of DEC-148 (1); its provenance tracking stays only as that pruning aid. (2) **Bounded, never silent.** If the joint enumeration of undecided cells exceeds a configured bound, the branch is reported as **unjudged**, in its own count, and is not passed. The PR gate requires zero unjudged; the nightly reports the count, and a non-zero count is a finding to triage. (3) **Advisory allocation follows Core** (CP3.2-3): `AllocateFileRange` is advisory (`util/fs_helpers.cpp`), so a size floor is recorded only when the allocation actually established it. A successful no-op allocation under an already larger file still raises it. (4) **Sanctioned messages follow Core per call path:** activation and ConnectTip read failures use `Failed to read block` (`validation.cpp:3409`); the startup-replay caller uses `Unable to replay blocks. You will need to rebuild the database using -reindex-chainstate.` (`node/chainstate.cpp:251`). This corrects 436a763e's normalization of the replay path. DEC-161's sanctioned fail-stop set uses the same table. (5) The existing positive controls (Deriver plain and sync-first, partial overwrite) must pass under (a′) or (b), and Sol's CopyFinalizer, FloorFinalizer and SizeDeriver probes become regressions. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-163 | **E0-04: every Sent row is written in one SQLite transaction together with the artifact's full marker set; the erase window shrinks to memory reconciliation** (DW E0-04.P2a Sol r4 R4-F1; the fourth marker-durability round after r1 H1, R2-F1 and R3-F1, so the invariant moves into a single backend operation). (1) **Atomic Sent bundle.** Every Sent (or MaybeSent) write for a marked artifact goes through one backend call that writes the artifact's **complete current marker set**, cached Durable markers included, and the final row in **one SQLite transaction**. That covers first and repeat sightings, admission repair, retries, erase repair and close retry. The step identities are gathered under J; the SQLite I/O runs outside J. Under DEC-160's DELETE, either order then leaves markers and Sent together. Sol checked 1,440 file-backed orderings on the real SQL. (2) **The erase window loses its evidence role.** Recording a sighting as Sent-owed because an erase is running, pausing NotSent writes, and joining Sent writers before the DELETE are all removed. Sightings use the ordinary atomic bundle with its ordinary failed-write obligation. (3) **Kept, for memory and lifecycle only:** the removal barrier; the in-flight `register` join (DEC-134); counting the blocking delete for close; the delete owning its outcome after the caller is cancelled; and a narrow publication and cleanup guard, or artifact operation versions, so that an erased ID returned by the DELETE never discards newer pending or Durable state in memory. Copy deferral stays only if that guard needs it, and the design says why. (4) R4-F1's probe (`review_r4_failed_committed_erase_then_sighting_before_any_copy`) becomes a regression that reopens before any repair. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-164 | **No storage write may wait without observing the engine's background error** (CH2a a3 Opus high O1). With `allow_stall = true`, a RocksDB writer blocked on the write buffer manager is never woken when the background flush fails, for example on a full disk. The node then hangs silently, where Core's LevelDB writer re-checks the background error and aborts with "System error while flushing". Rule: every engine write path, in `dashd-store` and in anything built on it, either cannot block, or blocks only in a loop that observes `check_background` and trips the fail-stop latch on a background error. The concrete mechanism is the implementer's choice: write with `no_slowdown`, then on "Write stall" check the background error and back off and retry; or another design that meets the rule. The regression test is the portable case (read-only DB directory, writable `wal_dir`): the write must return an error and trip the latch within a bounded time, never hang. Same spirit as DEC-62 and ST1's fail-stop: a stalled node is worse than an aborted one. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-165 | **ST3a CP3.3 rulings: recovering after a reference fail-stop is allowed; the extended nested component is sampled; startup-replay messages follow Core per path** (ST3a CP3.3, the implementer's three open items). (1) **Option (B), not (A).** When the uninterrupted reference recovery ends in a sanctioned fail-stop, and the interrupted branch instead completes, the branch is **allowed**, counted in its own `recovered-after-failstop` count, if and only if its completed state passes every non-R7a check (D1 durability, R1–R8, the projection checks). This is Core's behaviour: a fail-stop needs operator action, and a later start can succeed when the data reads. (A) would add per-choice outcome metadata on the recovery's own cells, the same inference DEC-162 removed. If a completed branch fails any other check, that is a finding. (2) **`nested_depth1_extended` is sampled, nightly only.** Inner images over the 4,096-branch cap are explored up to the cap, then by a deterministic seeded uniform sample of the rest (seed recorded in `summary.txt`). The sampled count is reported apart and does not fail `is_clean`. Every PR-gate and other nightly component stays exhaustive under its cap, and truncation there still fails. (3) **Startup-replay engine errors use Core's message for that call path,** cited at v24.0.0-rc.3 the way DEC-162 (4) does (replay, ConnectTip, activation and open each map to the string Core produces). Don't sanction a message Core doesn't emit on that path. Extend the per-path table with a citation for each row. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-166 | **CH2b erratum E-b1 accepted, except D16: Core's `ChainStateFlushed` is signalled only after B1 is durable, never at the freeze** (CH2b b1 commit 1, fd1fdaae0). (1) **D16 is reversed.** The freeze-time signal would let the wallet's saved locator run ahead of the recovered chainstate after a crash between the freeze and B1. Core never allows that: its `FlushStateToDisk` writes the coins synchronously before `ChainStateFlushed` (`validation.cpp`). So V hands the signal to the `FlushSignals` port when it observes W's release of that flush, after B1 returned durable. It still carries the locator captured at `at_site` (the pre-step tip's). The signal may then reach the queue *after* later `BlockConnected` events, so the wallet's locator can lag what it has processed. That is conservative: at worst a restart rescans a few more blocks, and `findLocatorFork` covers it. The locator is never ahead of durable state. Record this ordering difference as the new D16 (it 'lags, never leads'), with a CRASH-suite assertion that the locator handed to the port is never above B1's durable tip. The ALWAYS shortcut signals after its index-only write returns, as written. (2) **E-b1.3's move of step 7 into CH1-node's step-4 slot is accepted.** The scan unlinks only files already marked pruned. (3) **O2's step-11 reorder for copy-point flushes only is accepted.** Bk → `after_bk` → snapshot → drop `Arc` → `FlushDone`. The extra wait applies only to rare RPC copy points. (4) Every other part of E-b1 (E-b1.1, .2, .4's port and subscriber split, .5–.8) is accepted as written. (5) **Assertion refinement (accepted, Core parity):** the CRASH assertion is that the port's locator names `new` or one of its ancestors, except at a disconnect-site freeze, where it may name `new`'s child `d`. That is exactly Core's behaviour: `DisconnectTip` flushes at `parent(d)` (`v:3303`) and then signals `GetLocator()` before `SetTip` (`v:3316`). And it is handed only after that flush's B1 returned Durable. Ours is never further ahead than Core's. CH1-node §7.1 carries the matching erratum (staging moves from `freeze_after_step` to W's release). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-167 | **ChainLock enforcement vectors assert Core's fixed point; DEC-147's idempotence assumption is withdrawn** (HX1 finding 2). Core's EnforceBlock pass resets failure flags only when the locked block itself is flagged (validation.cpp:4075). A first pass can therefore change nothing, and a later pass (dashd always runs one, through the rescheduled update and the 5 s timer) can reconnect the branch. So a repeat is not always idempotent. Rule: (1) SB4 ports Core's **single pass exactly**, with no fixpoint shortcut inside the pass. (2) The driver re-runs enforcement the way dashd does, until a pass changes nothing. (3) A vector asserts the trace up to the first enforcement boundary of each lock (as in DEC-147) **and the state at the fixed point**. A state after an intermediate pass is an allowed transient, recorded but not asserted. (4) A schedule whose fixed point differs is a finding, as before. Same spirit as DEC-95/DEC-138/DEC-147: Core's semantics, not one sample of its timing. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-168 | **E0-10a failed R7 at platform's pin; desktop 1.0 ships DASHPAY §2.2's degraded mode, and E0-10c is scoped as follows.** (1) The desktop graph gets rust-dashcore #1094, #1073 and #1102 through **platform #5307** (the v5.1 move), not a backport onto `40268cc0`. It is pinned by rev on pasta's platform fork (DEC-18) until #5307 merges upstream. (2) **dash-spv: the first quorum mined after start must reach `Verified`** when its commitment arrives (today `Skipped(MissedList)` for 7–11 h, at the pin and at `dev`). The fix goes on a rust-dashcore `dev` branch; an upstream PR opens only after the manager approves its text. (3) **A quorum-key lookup that returns the status.** platform-wallet's `SpvRuntime::get_quorum_public_key` drops it today; this is proposed upstream to platform and carried on the fork pin in the meantime (DEC-18). (4) **Chain anchoring of quorum lists (public dashpay/rust-dashcore#1117) is outside E0-10c**; R7 enforcement (DP3-04/H-09 full mode) waits for it. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-169 | **CANDIDATE-UNLINKED (private, PLAN §7) is a liveness defect, not consensus; the Rust node does not replicate it.** The private Opus assessment rates it Low: a per-node stall that clears on restart, with no attacker-only path found. Rule: (1) SB4/CH1-node implement Core's semantics **plus the fix recorded in the private file**, as a registered deviation under the codename (quirk register, no mechanism in tracked text); (2) the SB4 harness keeps treating Core's signature as a private known finding and never asserts it as a vector; (3) disclosure to Dash Core is pasta's decision (the recommendation is a private low-severity report); until then, nothing about it goes upstream. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-170 | **SB1 gates: mechanisms, not more plant-by-plant fixes** (Sol r2, second round of the same finding classes). (1) **L7 reference is immutable.** It is the size record committed at the merge base with node/integration. When there is none (bootstrap), it is a constant in `layers.toml` (12,615,752 B: libdash_spv_ffi.so, aarch64-linux-android, release profile, NDK 29.0.14206865, API 24; citing this DEC), never `HEAD` and never a record on the branch. A change to the constant needs a new DEC id in the same commit; the checker refuses otherwise (R2-1). (2) **Rules are keyed by package name and applied to every resolved package ID with that name**, workspace or external, path, git or registry. That covers hot-path, layer and pure flags alike, with no member-only loops (R2-2; same class as r1 M1/M2). (3) **Allocator and thread checks judge the linked product, not its inputs.** For each S/P crate, build a minimal consumer binary (host and Android, features on and off) that links the crate through its normal linkage. Fail if the product defines a user global allocator (`__rg_alloc` / a non-default `__rust_alloc` provider). Build-only and proc-macro units are excluded by construction. The text and rlib scans are retired (R2-3, R2-5; third round of r1 M6). (4) **Per-target graphs come from cargo's own platform filter** (`cargo metadata --filter-platform <triple>` / `cargo tree --target`), not by mapping displayed pairs back to all resolved edges (R2-4). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-171 | **DP1-02 registration flows: mechanisms instead of a third round of patches** (Sol r1 on Phase A: 1 blocker, 7 majors, after two Opus rounds that had already fixed issues of the same kinds). (1) **One owner per row.** A row is driven only by the holder of its claim, which carries a persisted generation. Discard, resume, cleanup and Finish are requests to that owner, or they take the claim (bumping the generation). Every write checks the generation, so a stale driver, a duplicate guard or a retired cleanup cannot act (R1-02, R1-08). (2) **The identity index is a persisted field of the row**, fixed at creation, never synthesized. An identity is adopted only after ownership is proven: the id derives from the row's own outpoint (R1-01, R1-05). (3) **Unknown means stop, conservatively.** A row or encoding this build cannot fully interpret is never funded, discarded or advanced, and `funds_committed` is true whenever any lock or proof exists. The whole plan, including the profile encoding, is validated before funding (R1-04, R1-06). (4) **Import classifies by evidence.** A row that arrives with a tracked outpoint is Funded, whatever its stored state, and Finish and resume can take it from there (R1-03). (5) **Funding uses the wallet's real spendable set**, excluding manually locked coins and host reservations, through the same coin-control path as E0-04.P2b's TxDraft exclusion (R1-07). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-172 | **SB4 harness: address-sensitivity is detected by perturbed replay, not predicted** (HX1 Sol r2 R2-1, the second miss in the cut predicate after R1-3). Core decides some ties by pointer order (`m_failed_blocks`, loaded-block sequence ids). Rule: (1) a cheap, conservative pre-filter (at least two entries in `m_failed_blocks`, or a loaded-block tie, anywhere in the sequence) marks a sequence *possibly sensitive*; (2) a possibly sensitive sequence is replayed at least twice more under distinct heap-perturbation seeds (random dummy allocations interleaved with block-index allocations), and any difference in outcome, state or trace cuts it; (3) the predicate alone can neither accept nor cut a sequence. A recorded vector must be stable under perturbation. Same spirit as DEC-147/167: assert Core's semantics, never one sample of its memory layout. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-173 | **DP1-02: one registration actor per wallet; ambiguity parks; coin policy is fenced through selection** (Sol r2: R2-01 and R2-03 are concurrent-writer races that DEC-171's generation checks only partly closed, R2-02 is the unknown-row variant of the index class, R2-04 is a snapshot-to-signing race). (1) **All `dp_registration` reads that lead to a write, and all writes, go through one per-wallet registration actor**: a single task owning the rows, with a message queue. Drivers, the changeset tap, Finish, discard, resume and cleanup send it requests; claim acquisition is an actor message, so no blocking closure can overtake a newer claim. The persisted generation stays, as a crash-safety check, and a driver that loses it stops (no endless `Again`) (R2-01, R2-03). (2) **Ambiguity parks.** An unattributed recovered lock that an unreadable, outpoint-less row could own is never consumed or imported through another row. It counts as possible funding, and that row's `funds_committed` is true, until positive evidence settles who owns it (R2-02). (3) **Coin policy is fenced through selection.** The locked/reserved check and input selection/signing run under one coin-policy fence shared with `lock_outpoints`, dust locks and spend reservations, or the builder receives the authoritative exclusion set read under that fence. E0-04.P2b's TxDraft coin control is the intended home; the interim refusal must hold until signing (R2-04). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-174 | **SPV active-chain windowing (SB4 A-31): the writer windows; a deep disconnect re-extends from the store** (SB4 C9b: A-31 holds 14.30 MB against its 4.375 MB gate, because nothing calls `set_base`; D-C4-7 left it to SB5 and neither design names the call). (1) In SPV mode the **writer** moves the base to `max(anchor floor, tip_height − w)` after every committed step, inside SB4's step-commit path. SB5's segmented activation calls the same function per released segment; it does not own a separate rule. (2) A disconnect below the base never asserts. It **re-extends the active chain from the store** by ancestry (stable ids, cold records, the `TipHost` view of SB4 §7.3) before popping. Reorgs below the SPV floor stay refused (DEC-119 R7-O1). (3) A-31's 4.375 MB gate stands and is measured with the window in place. The live residual of about 4.0 MB against §3.8's 3.5 MB estimate is accepted inside the gate, and §3.8 gets an erratum with the measured figure. Owners: SB4 implements (1) and (2) in `ActiveChain` and the writer; SB5 F3 adds the per-segment call. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-175 | **SB4 harness: every random sequence gets the perturbed replay; no pre-filter** (HX1 Sol r3 R3-1, the third miss in the address-sensitivity class: a live `precious` tie with a reset negative sequence id differs under up/down with an empty pre-filter mark). Amends DEC-172 (1): the pre-filter is removed as a gate. Every generated sequence (random smoke, random-delivery and regen) runs under at least the fixed `up` and `down` heap perturbations, and any difference in outcome, state or trace cuts it at the earliest differing step, the minimum across all channels (R3-2). The address-sensitivity mark stays only as a diagnostic label in the output. Sol r3 showed every index pointer that orders these ties lives in the perturbed allocation region at this pin, so replay is complete for supported operations; a re-pin re-runs the allocation audit. Cost (about 3× harness time per sequence) is accepted over a fourth predicate round. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-176 | **SB1 allocator rule: a source-level ban over the all-features closure, not feature sampling** (Sol r4 R4-1, the third miss in the allocator class after R3-2: a `default-features = false` consumer selecting one feature installs a cfg-gated allocator that the all/default/no-features probes miss). Rule: features only add dependencies, so the `--all-features` resolved closure of every shared (S) and pure-named crate, per target (host, aarch64-linux-android), is a superset of every product configuration's closure. The gate scans the library sources of every package in that closure (the directory tree of each lib target's `src_path`, including generated `OUT_DIR` sources where present) for the `global_allocator` token, regardless of any `cfg`. Any hit fails, unless the package is on an allow-list keyed by name and version with a DEC citation; the list starts empty. The linked-consumer probe stays as a sanity check, but it is not the proof. This supersedes the allocator part of DEC-170 (3). | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-177 | **O2a-EV's O8 cross-check moves to O8's acceptance** (O2a-EV Sol r1 finding 1). PLAN row O2a-EV asks for `oracle_mnlist_canonical` compared with O8's `getcanonicalmnlist` at ≥20 heights, but O8 (M2) has not started, so that comparison is an external prerequisite, not an O2a-EV gate item. O2a-EV's gate rests on its codec vectors, the 200-block four-leg comparison and EV2a's L1 (20 mainnet lists imported from Core's evodb with real internal ids and registration counts, hashed by the oracle). **O8's acceptance gains:** record `getcanonicalmnlist` at ≥20 heights per network (mainnet ≤ 2,090,000) and compare with `oracle_mnlist_canonical` and with dash-dml's snapshot bytes; any mismatch reopens O2a-EV. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-178 | **The node's P2P decode verdicts are judged by the Core oracle, and Core-lenient reads live at the node decode boundary** (SB6 Sol r1 F2, the second occurrence of the bool-leniency class after `sendcmpct`). (1) For every SB6 decode-policy row that reads a payload, accept/reject is compared with O2c's `oracle_p2p_decode` (Core's own deserializers) over the row's corpus and fuzz inputs. A hand-written LENIENT list is no longer evidence; it may stay only as a re-encode allow-list. A Throw where Core accepts is a divergence. (2) Where the pinned base-sdk is stricter than Core (BSX catalogue class (c): A-12 `CoreBool` and its instances, such as D-11 `sendcmpct` and `getqrinfo` `extra_share`), the node's decode boundary in `dash-p2p` reads the field Core's way, until the base-sdk fork gains the leniency (BL-82). (3) No peer is scored for a Core-accepted alternate. | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-179 | **Desktop coin reservations are owner-keyed** (DP1-02 Sol r4 R4-01, the second miss in the reservation class after R3-01: cleanup of a stale registration erased a later send's reservation on the same coin, because `PendingSpends` is one per-wallet set). Rule: the per-wallet reservation store maps each outpoint to a set of **owners** (an operation token: registration row and lock, `PreparedTx` id, CoinJoin session). Every reserve and release names its owner, and a release removes only that owner's claim. A coin is protected while any owner holds it. Registration reconciliation removes only registration-owned claims for untracked locks. Sends and CoinJoin get owner tokens now; this is minimal, P2b's full TxDraft migration stays P2b's. Policy snapshots read "has any owner". | 2026-10-10 |
| DEC-180 | **CH2b quirk 44: an undecodable `head_blocks` record fails the load** (CH2b b1; registers the manager's earlier c5 item-8 ruling). Core's `CDBWrapper::Read` returns false on a decode failure, so its `GetHeadBlocks` treats a corrupt `head_blocks` value as absent and the chainstate as consistent (dbwrapper.h:322–326, txdb.cpp:105–111, validation.cpp:5230). That can skip a replay the node needs. The Rust node instead reports corruption and fails the load. It is a deliberate, registered deviation (quirk 44, with a test) on a corrupt-store path only: no peer-visible or consensus difference on a healthy store. The CH2 design's quirk table (CH2-coins-flush.md, near line 1720) lists it. | 2026-10-11 |
| DEC-181 | **SB1 allocator proof = a canary-allocator link of every shipped product configuration** (Sol r5, the fourth miss in the allocator class: `#[path]`/macro-assembled allocators and downstream dependency-feature unification escape any static source/closure analysis; the allow-list matched by substring). Supersedes DEC-176's proof (the scan stays as an early warning). (1) `layers.toml` gains a **product registry**: every artifact the program ships or tests as a product, each with its exact feature selection and target triples. That covers the node binary(ies), `dash-spv-ffi` and `key-wallet-ffi` (host and aarch64-linux-android), and any other `cdylib`/`staticlib`/bin we publish. Adding a product means adding a registry row, and a gate check fails on any workspace `cdylib`/`staticlib`/bin that is not in the registry. (2) **The proof:** for each registry row the gate builds the product as configured, plus a canary that defines its own `#[global_allocator]` (through a test-only cfg the product honours, or a thin canary crate linking the product's lib with the same features). The canary link must succeed. A `conflicts with global allocator in: <crate>` failure names the offending dependency. It is complete for shipped products by construction, whatever the macros, paths or feature unification. A product that intentionally installs an allocator declares it in its registry row, and the canary for that row is replaced by a check that the conflict names only the product's own crate. (3) Consumers outside the registry are out of scope; a shared crate's docs say so. (4) **The source scan stays, as an advisory early warning, not the proof.** (5) **The allow-list (R5-3)** uses exact structured identity: a `[[allocator.exception]]` record with `package`, `version` and `dec` fields, and the cited DEC row must contain the exact token `package@version`. No substring matching. Add distinct-name and distinct-version prefix controls. | 2026-10-11 |
| DEC-182 | **dash-dml's unique-property map keeps SipHash; the 1.2× probe bound is waived** (EV2a fix r1, the DEC-50(3) one-time comparison). Measured instructions per operation: on normal keys SipHash probes cost 412 vs Fx 186 (2.22×, failing the 1.2× bound), and add/remove cost 1.04×. On 512 Fx-colliding keys Fx degrades to 13,756 per probe and ~270k per add, while SipHash stays at 388; OrdMap costs 1.16–1.93× SipHash. The map is fed by peer-supplied ProTx fields, so adversarial-input resistance decides. The probe's absolute cost (≈400 instructions) is negligible against block processing. Rule: SipHash stays; the bound is replaced by "adversarial within 2× of normal", which SipHash meets. | 2026-10-11 |
| DEC-183 | **Desktop trust provenance is bound to content** (E0-10b H2: a `dp_trust_verified` record outlived a later library replacement of the entity with fallback-sourced data). Rule: each `dp_trust_verified` record stores a digest of the entity's canonical bytes as verified (identity, profile document, DPNS label document; the digest names its encoding version). A reader treats an entity as verified only if its current digest matches the record. A mismatch reads as unverified, and the re-fetch path may re-verify. No change-path clearing is needed or relied on; that is the remove-not-filter rule, applied by construction. The dw-appdb migration adds the column, and old rows without a digest read as unverified. E0-10b's other seven checkpoint decisions are accepted as reported: writes-wait goes to H-09; a disagreeing held key refuses; Degraded serves an SPV-Verified key when the trusted service is down; no rebuild from storage; masternode sync is sticky per session (headers included); the re-fetch covers identity, profile and DPNS label; a quorum refusal maps to `context_unavailable`. | 2026-10-11 |
| DEC-184 | **Fuzz gates are "ran until plateau with 0 divergences", not CPU-hour budgets** (pasta, 2026-10-11, fuzz CPU policy). (1) **Plateau rule.** A farm leg is *plateaued* when, over its own last **60 min** of fuzzing time (window configurable down to 15 min), libFuzzer's `cov` gained 0 edges **and** `ft` grew by ≤ 0.1 % (at least one full window of fuzzing must have run). `fuzzctl farm` stops a plateaued leg, records `"plateau": {...}` and `status: "plateaued"` in its verdict.json, and gives its cores to legs still growing. A target that never grows is killed or reworked, never left burning CPU. (2) **Gates.** Every gate, DEC, BL row or PRD/PLAN line that asks for ≥ N CPU-h clean (NFR-CEQ-04's 24/72 CPU-h, PLAN §2 L1, the M-exit rows for CS1, BS4b/BS6b/BS7/BS8, `diff_chain` O2b, BL-37/39/41/55, D14's 75 CPU-h for P2a-net, EV2 §6.3's 24 CPU-h) now reads **"ran until plateau (DEC-184 (1)) with 0 divergences and 0 crashes"**. The per-PR 5-minute smoke is unchanged. **Gate certification always uses the 60-minute window**; a shorter window (down to 15 min) may stop a leg to save CPU, but that stop alone doesn't certify the gate (CS1b Sol r2). A CPU-hour figure is reported but is not a goal. (3) **Rework, not more hours.** A plateau at low coverage triggers a coverage review (which reachable Rust/Core code the target never hits), then new seeds, dictionaries, structure-aware mutators or new targets — not a longer run (BL-87). (4) **Priority.** Farm units run at `Nice=19`, `IOSchedulingClass=idle`, `CPUWeight=10` (fuzzctl farm launch; FZ1). (5) **Applied 2026-10-11 03:30Z:** `cs1b-r2` (56.8 CPU-h), `o2c-overlay-r2` (117.6 CPU-h) and `bs6b-r1` (27.3 CPU-h) stopped, all 0 divergences / 0 crashes. Over each leg's last hour every cs1b-r2 and bs6b-r1 leg and 12 of 14 o2c legs gained ≤ 1 edge and ≤ 0.1 % ft; they count as plateaued with 0 divergences for CS1, BS6b and O2c acceptance. o2c's `diff_p2p_mnlistdiff` (+10 cov, +1.1 % ft) and `diff_p2p_qrinfo` (+0.3 % ft) were still growing and resume under FZ1's plateau rule. **Correction (same day):** CS1's receipt, measured per leg over its 4 parallel jobs, gives `diff_sigops` +1 edge / +0.31 % ft and `diff_script_primitives` +0.12 % ft over the last 60 min, above the 0.1 % bound. CS1 Sol r2 judges the measurement; unless it rules them plateaued, those two legs run again under FZ1 until the plateau rule fires before CS1 merges. Likewise BS6b's `diff_payload_9` plateaued before its new seeds loaded and reruns under FZ1. The nightly `node-fuzz-farm-auto` (9.8 CPU-h, 0 divergences; its one `kv_model` finding is a slow-unit, BL-83) and its timer are stopped until FZ1 merges. |
| DEC-185 | **ST3a: a sampled remainder never certifies; the extended nested component is covered by sharded exhaustive enumeration; a returned error closes a path but is never an abort mark** (ST3a CP3.3 Sol, CP3.3-1/-2). (1) **Supersedes DEC-165 (2).** A per-cell seeded walk is not a uniform sample of the remaining leaves (Sol: a dense failing subtree holding 99.6 % of the remainder was reached with p = 2⁻¹⁶). A truly uniform sampler needs subtree sizes that cost a full run to learn, so none is attempted. A capped run's report is `truncated`, with or without sampling, and **a truncated component is never `clean`**. Sampling stays as a cheap smoke that can only find, never pass. (2) **Coverage mechanism.** The extended nested component is enumerated **exhaustively in shards**: the decision tree is split by a fixed-depth decision prefix into deterministic shards. Each shard runs to completion (uncapped) in a separate low-priority unit (Nice 19, idle IO, CPUWeight 10), and a ledger records shard completion. The component counts as clean only when every shard is complete with 0 findings at the gated head. Shards whose prefix the head's change can't reach may carry over from an earlier head only if the harness proves the prefix's code paths are unchanged; otherwise all shards rerun. (3) **CP3.3-1.** The harness must not synthesize `Mark::Abort`. A `Sut::open` error is a terminal outcome for path-balance ("path not closed") accounting only. R8 and the fail-stop classifications read the SUT's own trace, so an unmarked return after a fatal error fails R8. Add the unmarked-fatal-return negative next to the marked, wrong-message and late-write controls. |
| DEC-186 | **Fuzz-gate certification is a separate confirmation run; live plateau detection only stops legs** (FZ1 Sol r1: B1–B4 and M1/M2 show that certifying from live samples of concurrent worker logs has too many false-pass paths: per-worker maxima vs union, corpus-reload time, clock steps, missing verdicts, SIGINT outcomes, the starvation median). This is the second miss in the class, so the rule is a mechanism, not patches. (1) **Live plateau = stop heuristic.** The farm's live detection may stop a leg to save CPU, recording `plateau-suspected`. It **never** certifies. A cut slice is simply `interrupted`. The starvation median is removed; the live window may use any simple monotonic measure. (2) **Certification = `fuzzctl certify <target>@<pin>`.** At the gated head, merge the leg's corpora into a fresh dir. Then run one libFuzzer instance whose stats are a true union: `-fork=J`, if the differential sink and oracle work under fork mode (the parent prints union cov/ft), else a single process (`-jobs=1`). It runs on the merged corpus for 60 min (`-max_total_time=3600`), at farm priority. Baseline = cov/ft at `INITED` after the whole merged corpus loads; result = the final stats line. It **certifies** iff: cov gain 0; ft gain ≤ 0.1 %; 0 divergences, crashes and sink failures, read fail-closed from run.sh's own verdict (a missing or torn verdict ⇒ not certified); a clean libFuzzer exit; and the process tree's rusage CPU ≥ 80 % of J × 3600 s, measured with a monotonic clock (else `starved`, rerun). The certificate is derived from that evidence on read (invariants checked), never trusted as a stored boolean. **Amendment (FZ1 rework, same day):** node-sink refuses fork mode, so certify runs one process (J = 1, a true union) merged into the working corpus. The window is **3600 s of the process's own CPU time after INITED**, not wall time, so low priority (Nice 19) can't starve a certification; a 12 h wall cap gives `starved`. Certification also needs ≥ 10⁶ executions in the window, or a per-target `min_certify_execs`; otherwise the status is `insufficient-progress`. **Amendment 2 (FZ1 Sol r2, B1):** coverage is never read from a SIGINT'd run's last status line, because libFuzzer's interrupt path prints no final cov/ft. Before and after the window, measure the corpus by a **replay pass**: the same binary with `-runs=0` over the working corpus, whose `INITED` line gives cov/ft. Certification needs **equal replay cov and ≤ 0.1 % replay ft growth, and no `NEW` line anywhere in the window's log**. A torn or partial `NEW` counts as growth. Any missing replay, or a replay that fails, means not certified. The CPU window counts the fuzzer process's own CPU only, not reaped children. A finding open or unreadable at completion blocks success. **Amendment 3 (FZ1 Sol r3, B1–B3 and M1; the third round in the false-certification class, so mechanisms plus a termination rule).** (a) **Graceful stop at an iteration boundary.** The window ends with libFuzzer's own graceful-exit request (SIGUSR1, checked in `MaybeExitGracefully` at the mutation-loop boundary). An in-flight `ReportNewCoverage` therefore finishes publishing before exit. The run must show the graceful-exit message and exactly one complete final record: the `DONE` status line plus the final `stat::` block. If they don't arrive within a bounded time, the process is killed and the run is `failed`. A SIGINT/SIGKILL stop never certifies. *(Implementation, accepted 10-11: libFuzzer's SIGUSR1 path (`MaybeExitGracefully`) prints no cov or ft, so the window ends with `-stop_file` instead. That is checked at the same loop boundary, and it ends normally with the DONE line and the final stats, exit 0. A process that outlives the stop by 1,500 s is killed and gets `failed`.)* (b) **In-process cov/ft check.** The same process's `DONE` cov must equal its INITED cov, and its `DONE` ft must be ≤ INITED ft × 1.001. This is in addition to the equal-replay rule, not a replacement. (c) **Whole-log grammar.** Every byte after INITED must parse as a complete, newline-terminated line of an allow-listed libFuzzer or sink form. Each required final counter (`new_units_added`, `number_of_executed_units`, …) must appear exactly once, anchored, with nothing after the digits. A torn, duplicated, unterminated or unknown line is `failed`, never skipped. The units-added and NEW/REDUCE-lines balance stays as a further veto. (d) **One eligibility function.** A single fail-closed function decides whether a leg is eligible at a cutoff. It covers open and unreadable clusters, pending or untriaged runs, and every triage error, including a missing regression vector or unreadable provenance. `certify` uses it before and after the window, and the status reader uses it with the certificate's recorded completion cutoff, so the command and the status can't disagree. (e) **Termination.** Sol r4 checks (a)–(d) narrowly. After r4, a further finding in this class that needs injected faults inside libFuzzer's own I/O or scheduling is a documented BACKLOG residual and doesn't block the merge. A finding that certifies a natural, uninjected run, or that bypasses (a)–(d), still blocks. (3) This replaces the certification half of DEC-184 (1) and the FZ1 starvation guard. DEC-184's gate wording now means "a passing `certify` run at the gated head". The 60-min window and the 0 cov / 0.1 % ft thresholds are unchanged. (4) FZ1 keeps the BL-83/BL-86 fixes, the priority properties and `--only`. Shares: an `--only` launch splits the night's cap among the selected legs. No redistribution (M4). Private-route naming: name only positively public targets; unknown or removed ones count as private (M3). |
| DEC-187 | **SB5 SPV memory bounds corrected (SB5 F8 measurements, D71/D72).** (1) **Marks bound.** The SPV step's bound becomes cap + M(m) + L(m), where L(m) = 12·next_pow2(m) ≤ 24m bytes for the disconnected-id list. SB4 drops `EnforceReport.disconnected`'s duplicate in SPV mode, because `VirtualTip.disconnected` already holds it, so the list is held once. Measured at 50,000 marks: 1,465,336 B. (2) **No full plan in SPV stage 1.** Stage 1 must not build SB4's full `tip_plan`: Sol's witness alone costs 1.44 MB. Stage 3 uses `tip_target`, and each sub-step uses `tip_plan_bounded`. SB4 §9.1's stage list and SB14's driver say so. (3) **Typical totals.** SB0/SB5 §6.3's typical SPV totals are corrected to the measured bounds: **5.38 MB desktop (w = 10,000) and 2.21 MB mobile (w = 2,048)**, up from 4.42 and 1.58 MB. The three causes: an arena Entry is 240 B, not 200; the arena's fixed tables add 136 KiB; a decoded headers message (288 KB at B = 2,000) sits on the writer during accept. Both still fit the mobile envelope, and PF0's memory rows gate them from now on. **Amendment (SB5 D74):** SB4's `HashMap<BlockId, Mark>` overlay uses a 13 B bucket, not 9, so M(m) = 13·(b + b/2) + 32 ≤ 45m + 2,528, with b floored at 128. The marks account must size the table from its own type and assert it exactly. Measured: 440,824 ≤ 705,952 at 9k marks and 1,595,896 ≤ 2,254,240 at 50k. **Amendment 2 (SB5 fix r1, A-F12 attribution).** (a) The step's 1,024 B fixed term is replaced by derived terms, sized from the types: a **path term** for `ActiveChain`'s copy-on-publish (one leaf, the inner levels for u32 heights, two `Arc`s; the old path is freed at release, so both coexist) and a **registry term** 2·(Arc + Registry + max_feeds·RegistryEntry), with `max_feeds ≤ 64` validated at `Config`. (b) **The caller's batch is outside the step**, as §6.2 already says of the decoded peer message: A-F12 builds the `HeaderBatch` before the measured call, and any copy SB5 makes of it is charged. (c) **Arena chunk turnover and the hash index's rehash are residency (§6.3), not step.** The arena's ⌈(N_win + B)/1,024⌉ + 1 + S chunks (245,760 B each at 240 B/entry) already cover the chunk allocated in accept before eviction frees one. §6.3 gains an explicit **index-rehash transient**: the old table coexists with the new one during a resize. (d) **No gap between the budgets.** A-F12 adds a plain 2,000-header step at mobile and desktop defaults (and B = 500) under the counting allocator. It charges arena and index growth to a residency account and everything else to the step account, then asserts both: step ≤ cap (+ M + L), and residency peak ≤ §6.3's chunk formula + the rehash transient. The test prints the per-account attribution. A byte charged to neither account fails the test. **Amendment 3 (SB5 fix r1, D76–D79).** (a) Accepted as measured: the resident B-tree and `chunk_live` growth count as residency; the fresh active-chain nodes a reorg builds while the pre-step snapshot still holds the old ones count as residency (the retained-snapshot row), which gives 2 × `active_bytes`; the §6.3 index row is derived, with a rehash transient row. Typical totals become **≈ 5.83 MB desktop and 2.42 MB mobile (1.99 MB at B = 500)**, with measured typical peaks 5.02 and 2.08 MB. Step caps: 433,728 / 203,328 / 119,328 B. (b) `STEP_FIXED_BYTES` is derived from `size_of` too and asserted exactly, like the other terms. (c) **D79, cold-cache admission vs a misfiled id:** keep the by-hash admission. Add an issued-id watermark in `Shared`: admitting a record whose id is ≥ the watermark is `Corruption` and trips the latch. A missing *issued* id that is reached by hash isn't detected at admission; it is detected when a by-id read misses the cache. SB4's test is split to assert both. |
| DEC-188 | **SB1 allocator proof: final scope; r7 is the last round in this class** (SB1 Sol r6, the sixth round on the allocator rule). (1) **Ownership is identified by package id.** An `allocator = "own"` conflict must name the product's own **package id** (name + version + source), never a crate name, so a dependency that shares the crate name doesn't pass (R6-1). (2) **The registry scan covers every target kind**, including examples, benches and tests with crate-type cdylib, staticlib or bin. Each is registered or listed as excluded with a reason (R6-2). The bench driver rejects the cpu-profile + heap-profile combination; a combined row is registered only if it's needed (R6-5). (3) **Native allocators are covered by a native-code allow-list, not by a link proof.** Every package in a shared or pure crate's closure with a `links` key or a build script that compiles C/C++ must appear in `layers.toml [[native]]` with package, version and a one-line review note confirming it doesn't replace or interpose malloc/free. An unlisted native package fails. This replaces the package-name bans for interposition (R6-3); the bans stay as a cheap early check. (4) **The source scan is advisory**: it warns and doesn't fail, as DEC-181 said (R6-4). (5) **Termination.** Sol r7 checks (1)–(4) narrowly. Any further finding in the allocator-proof class is recorded as a documented residual in BACKLOG and doesn't block SB1's merge, unless it's a false pass on a registered product's real build. |
| DEC-189 | **Desktop UI-stack gate rulings (G-02/G-03 measurements, before G-04).** (1) **U3c on Windows.** WebView2's classic scroll bar gutter is a platform difference, not a layout defect. Boxes are compared with that gutter excluded, and the residual must be ≤ 2 px. The app does **not** draw its own scroll bars: native scroll bars keep platform behaviour and accessibility. (2) **U5 cold start.** agentbox is shared at load 100–270, so its wall-clock time isn't a fair measure (as in DEC-184/186). U5 cold start passes only if **both** hold: spawn-to-first-paint ≤ 1.5 s on a quiet CI runner (the median of the cold runs), and the process's CPU time to first paint ≤ 1.5 s on agentbox. agentbox wall-clock is still recorded. (3) **U5 memory.** Judged on the UI's own share; engine memory belongs to H-06 (DASHPAY: "the gate isolates the UI's own cost"). Where the engine shares the process and can't be separated, the UI cost measured over an engine-free baseline counts. (4) **Palette.** The WCAG-AA colour variants (G-02 44ed46a / d232857) are approved as a design-spec change. Both stacks use them. G-04 (the stack decision) follows once G-03's column is complete. Under these rules, G-02's Tauri passes U5 cold start (0.57–1.08 s on CI; under 1.1 s CPU), and G-03's SwiftCrossUI fails it (4.4 s CPU on agentbox, 1.54 s CPU on CI). |

| DEC-188 am. 1 | **SB1 R8-1: the rustc argv is allow-listed, not parsed for known-bad shapes** (Sol r8, a blocking false pass under DEC-188 (5): a config-supplied joined `--extern=alias=path` was skipped by the extern parser and passed the registered `dash-spv-bench` heap-profile row's real build). This is the eighth round in the class, and every miss so far was an argv spelling the parser didn't know, so the mechanism changes. (1) **One canonical tokenizer** turns every captured rustc unit argv (product and dependencies, from any source: Cargo, RUSTFLAGS, CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS, config rustflags, build scripts) into (option, value) pairs, accepting every spelling rustc accepts (`--opt val`, `--opt=val`, `-Xval`, `-X val`). (2) **Allow-list of options.** Each option must be in a reviewed set with a per-option rule: `--extern` (both forms; a path must be a Cargo artifact of a package id in the unit graph, else fail; a pathless extern must resolve to a Cargo-reported or sysroot std crate), `-L` (existing path-kind rules), `-C` keys individually allowed (`link-arg`/`link-args` native only), `--cfg`, `--check-cfg`, `--crate-*`, `--edition`, `--emit`, `--error-format`, `--json`, `--cap-lints`, `--target`, `--diagnostic-width`, `-W/-A/-D/-F`, and so on. **Any option not on the list fails closed** with its name and its source, including `--sysroot`, `-Z` and `@argfile` (closing the r8 sysroot residual by rejection). (3) Regression tests: the joined-extern plant from r8 and one test per allow-listed option spelling pair. (4) Sol r9 checks (1)–(3) only. DEC-188 (5) still governs: a further finding blocks only if it is a false pass on a registered product's real build **through an allow-listed option**. |
| DEC-192 | **CH1-node: a marked task's post never blocks (LO-9 completion).** Under LO-9, marked tasks (F1 pop/ack tasks, the `ValidationSignals` thread and its listeners, W, shared EV-gate holders) may *post* to V. A blocking `send` into V's bounded channel is itself a wait on V (V must drain it), so it can deadlock while V waits on that task in `limit_queue` or an in-hold drain (CH1-node checkpoint 1, ask 3). Ruling: (1) `ChainHandle` has a second, **unbounded marked lane**. A send from a task carrying the no-wait marker goes there and never blocks or fails for capacity. Unmarked senders keep the bounded lane and its backpressure. (2) V serves both lanes under the same release-point and class rules, FIFO within each lane. No order is promised between lanes, as Core's `cs_main` promises none between waiters. (3) `try_send` with a capacity error is rejected, because it drops commands that Core would eventually run. (4) The lane's depth is a metric; marked tasks bound it by their own work. (5) Tests: a negative control that deadlocks with a bounded send from a marked task while V waits in `limit_queue`, the same scenario passing with the lane, and the case added to §9.6's lock-and-wait model. Also: CH1's internal table is renamed (`NodeBlockIndex`) so SB4's merged public `dash_chain::NodeIndex` keeps its name. |
| DEC-193 | **Superseded by DEC-196.** (manager, 2026-10-11; CH2b recovery tip selection with a loaded failed tip.) |
| DEC-194 | **P2b PA-2 is relaxed to the release-row authority** (manager, 2026-10-11; P2b Sol r7). A durable `funding_release` row commits before any vault signature; a released step with no signed lock reads MaybeSent (Repair NoKnownLock). Literal preventive PA-2 (no broadcast without a signed_lock row) needs a platform-fork change: abort the build when track_asset_lock returns an empty changeset, before the broadcaster. That is deferred to the next platform-fork bump as a desktop backlog item. Sol r7: no pin change is required for same-step no-double-pay. |

The quote stays E0-04's 100 000 000-credit bound for EnableDashPayKeys; tightening it is DP1-06's cost table. | DEC-196 | **CH2b recovery with a loaded FAILED coins tip** (manager, 2026-10-11; CH2b commit 6; supersedes DEC-193; reworded 2026-10-12 to describe V only). (1) A failed tip that does strictly more work than every valid block: V refuses to start (a sanctioned abort, model message per DEC-165 (3), no Core text; R6(e) accepts the abort). (2) Equal work: V reorgs to the valid equal-work tip. (3) Less work: normal activation. (4) Invalidating the tip or an ancestor and then crashing before the reorg flush is case (1): V refuses, as intended. The INVALID_TIP text now reads "…restart with -reindex, then repeat any invalidateblock calls (or use -reindex-chainstate, which keeps them, if -txindex is off)". |
| DEC-197 | **A released, unrecorded P2b funding step must not wedge DP1-02 registrations for good** (manager, 2026-10-11; DP1-02 Q15). The new draft takes a fresh index; the old row stays committed, non-discardable and adoptable. (1) P2b Repair adopting an asset lock from synced wallet history by the step's derived funding key was the other exit; **DEC-198 withdraws it**. Rationale: a broadcast lock is IS-locked within seconds, and an unbroadcast one's bytes died with the process. |
