| DEC-202 | **No durable coins tip means a logically empty store; tip-scoped effect records are kept** (manager, 2026-10-12; CH2b commit 6, as implemented in 46f6aa48d). With no durable tip, the coins and evo-state stores are treated as logically empty; coin and evo-state records, and `evo_tip`, found without a tip remain Corruption. The tip-scoped effect-CF records (`llmq_mined`, `llmq_mined_h`, `llmq_snapshot`) are KEPT, exactly as effect records above a durable tip are kept in the normal case; the forward replay from genesis rewrites them. Reasons: R13 (effects behind a completed barrier survive); A1 §2.10.1 (no less durable than Core, whose rotation snapshot is a raw write outside the EvoDB transaction); and the marker-with-old-null path never re-derives effects. Outcome: Core's clean start from genesis after a pre-first-flush kill. The tip and marker paths are unchanged. |

| DEC-209 | **Asserted-log rows get a `linked` status for a literal in rdashd's build whose emitter rdashd cannot reach yet** (manager, 2026-10-13; ND1 follow-merge onto db025b8be). CH1-node's `Failed to disconnect block` (feature_abortnode) and `Loaded best chain:` (feature_init) entered rdashd's closure with V, but rdashd has no header store (N7) and no `invalidateblock` RPC, so it cannot log either where the FTs expect it; `implemented` would be untrue and `pending` trips DEC-204 (3). (2) The ratchet accepts `linked` while an active literal holds the fragment and fails when none does (stale), so the record cannot outlive its literal. | DEC-210 | **Desktop wallet removal is enforced in app.sqlite by a per-process removal tombstone, not by fencing every writer** (manager, 2026-10-13; DP2-04 fix round 4, option B). Wallet removal must leave no wallet-scoped rows whatever the writer's timing, and some writers run synchronously inside the library's persister call, where they can't await or block on a fence. (1) `delete_wallet` records the wallet id in a `removed_wallets` table in the same transaction as its deletes. `BEFORE INSERT` triggers with `RAISE(IGNORE)` on every wallet-scoped table, and on settings_kv for the wallet scope and its `local:` scope, ignore rows for a tombstoned wallet; SQLite's write serialisation orders every writer, async or sync, before or after the removal. (2) Import and restore delete the tombstone before their first write. (3) Tombstones last one process lifetime only: session open clears the table, because no job survives a restart. They are never exported to `.dwbackup`, so no removed wallet stays recorded at rest. (4) The round-3 fence stays for in-memory state (the private-details cache); app.sqlite writers need no new fence. (5) Accepted residual: a late write from a removed wallet's old instance can land after a re-import of the *same* wallet. That is the same wallet's own data, so nothing crosses wallets. The contract documents it. (6) Rows platform-wallet-storage persists through a stale wallet handle are out of DP2-04's scope. DP2-04 reports, from reading the code, whether any library task can persist after `persister.delete_wallet`; if one can, it is fixed with the same tombstone idea at the next platform-fork bump (desktop backlog), as with DEC-206. **(3a) refinement, accepted 2026-10-13:** the clear runs right after the session's persister claim on the data dir, not in `AppDb::open`, and forgets only removals made by earlier processes; a process remembers its own removals per database file (detached app.sqlite jobs can outlive `close()`), and `readmit_wallet` forgets them. **(3b) amendment, 2026-10-13 (DP2-04 Sol r5):** (3a)'s process-local memory is removed. Every `AppDb` holds a shared OS file lock (std `File::lock_shared`) on a sidecar lock file next to app.sqlite for as long as any of its connections lives, so detached jobs keep it held after `close()`. Session open clears the tombstones only if a non-blocking exclusive `try_lock` on that file succeeds, i.e. no other process and no earlier instance in this process can still write; otherwise it keeps them. Skipping the clear is always safe; it only keeps a tombstone until a later clean open. Wallet lifecycle operations (seed and watch-only import, restore, removal, forget-secret) run under one engine-wide async lifecycle lock, from readmission through registration, store and flush to the failure path. A failed import of either kind records the removal again while the wallet is unregistered. | 2026-10-13 |
| DEC-211 | **A prune selection is revalidated by V against its current tip when V answers the clears, and V declines a selection the tip has fallen behind** (manager, 2026-10-13; CH2b b2 Opus high m1). CH2 selects the files to prune from the tip as of the flush's start (machine.rs:513). Core runs `FindFilesToPrune`/`FindFilesToPruneManual` under cs_main inside `FlushStateToDisk`, so it always prunes against the current tip and an `invalidateblock` deeper than `MIN_BLOCKS_TO_KEEP` can never leave the new tip with pruned block or undo data within 288 blocks. Rule: the `PruneClears` request carries the selection's bound (the highest height any selected file holds, plus the `MIN_BLOCKS_TO_KEEP` / manual-height rule it was chosen under); V checks it against its own tip before clearing and declines if any selected file is no longer prunable at that tip. On a decline W skips the prune for this flush (no clears, no unlink, Bp not written) and re-arms `fCheckForPruning`, so the next flush selects again. Same rule for b3's prune locks. Chosen over a D-row because the 288-block guarantee is an observable Core invariant (a reorg back would need re-download). | 2026-10-13 |
| DEC-212 | **A failed blocks-directory fsync is non-fatal, as in Core (BL-124, option a).** Core's `DirectoryCommit` (util/fs_helpers.cpp:153) ignores a failed directory fsync, so ST2's `FlatFileSeq::flush` makes one directory-sync attempt, logs "Failed to sync the blocks directory" and continues, with no retry (Core makes one attempt). fdatasync failures stay fatal. ST3a follows Core: FS sync-dir leaves R8's fatal set (`fail_stop_scan` plus §4.4 text). R1 and D1a entitle a durable reference into a file whose directory entry a failed sync-dir left pending in that epoch, because that is Core's own exposure. The log line is a D-row. Rejected: (b), keeping the barrier's dir fsync fatal, which is stricter than Core (DEC-95/99/102: simplify to Core semantics). | 2026-10-13 |
| DEC-213 | **While node workloads can't run on PR code (DEC-76 b), a PF0 ERROR whose only ERROR rows are the DEC-76(b) node-workload refusals counts as PASS for merging** (manager, 2026-10-13; train 9 merge). Train 9 brought ND1's `rdashd`, so from now on every gate whose head has the binary gets ERROR rows for `node-startup`, `node-mempool-accept`, `node-gbt` and `node-rpc-getblock` ("node workloads are refused on PR code"). This is by design: until counting is external and the data root is a per-gate copy, PR code must not touch shared state. Conditions: (1) every non-node row passes the normal rules (no regression, noisy-unresolved, drift or unapproved bench change); (2) every ERROR row's text is the DEC-76(b) refusal, and any other ERROR still blocks; (3) the merge message cites DEC-213. Node rows aren't gated on PR code meanwhile, so a node-level regression could merge unseen. BL-128 closes this by making node workloads safe to run on PR code (external counting plus a per-gate data-root copy, DEC-76 b), after which this exception lapses and node rows gate memory signals per DEC-52(4). | train 9 PF0 | PF0, every merge |

| DEC-229 | **LO-9 amendment (CH1-node DEC-156 (1)): the net actor is a V-waitable task under P3's C5.** V may wait for SyncSignal queue space (P3 D20/C5) only at release points, and never while `holding` (DEC-196 (2) startup hold) or before the net actor's first drain. | DEC-272 | **DEC-196 (2) after the load-time failure reset (manager, 2026-10-19; CH1-node D64).** DEC-196 (2) governs a tip that is still FAILED when startup's ABC runs. At `-checklevel >= 3`, `VerifyLoadedChainstate`'s `ResetBlockFailureFlags(nullptr)` clears the flag first, as Core does, so the loaded tip T is valid again. Where Core reaches that state, its LoadChainTip prune kept a tied valid block W that outranks T, so the ABC moves to W in Core and V alike, and DEC-196 (2) is unchanged. In V's own extension, where T outranks every tied block and Core asserts (`v:3592`), V's reading is accepted: T stays the tip and V applies Core's candidate prune against the tip right after a reset that reached it, so CheckBlockIndex holds. A reset that misses the tip prunes nothing, and D36's fallback stays. **Amended 14:35Z (Opus r1 F1):** the prune runs only when the reset leaves the tip a candidate. A ChainLock-conflicting T keeps its mark (the load-time reset does not ignore ChainLocks), stays a non-candidate, and keeps D36's fallback, so startup's ABC moves off it as at level ≤ 2. At `-checklevel <= 2` there is no reset and DEC-196 (2) applies as written. | Core has no defined outcome in the extension (it asserts). The reset is Core's own semantics, and the prune is Core's prune, so following both is the closest reading. Aborting there would make a valid-again tip refuse to start. |

| DEC-291 | **BL-250 closed: no arc_swap upstream PR (manager, 2026-10-20).** CH1's `perfgate measure --label bl250` matrix (evidence /work/scratch/ch1-node/bl250/table-bl250.md, measurement branches local only) shows every inline hint, in arc_swap or in dash-chain's `Arena::with`, moves unrelated rows by 2–8% in both directions. Inlining arc_swap's store path buys ≈1.5% on status-publish-coalesce/feed-subscribe-at and costs up to +6.6% on chain-ancestor-random. Region-exact callgrind puts all of that +454k Ir/iter on `Arena<Evicting>::with` losing its inlining into the ancestor walk, with no arc_swap code involved. The counts follow LLVM's inlining budget, so no PR. A debt-free store path would mean replacing `ArcSwap` with our own publication primitive, which is a design change and out of scope. DEC-275-style callgrind attribution stays the tool for store-row sway. |
| DEC-292 | **P3 S6b D120: adapter-level redo after `SnapshotExpired` (manager, 2026-10-20).** Independent Opus-h r1 (reviews/P3-S6b-r1-opus.md) judged each of the seven call sites. (1) Redoing only the adapter's read on the newest view is **accepted** at the six sites the review found safe: cmpctblock have_data/nTx, post-ProcessBlock IsValid(TRANSACTIONS), the direct-fetch walk HAVE_DATA, FindNextBlocksToDownload, direct-fetch IsValid(TREE) and pprev IsValid(CHAIN). Each mixed answer equals Core's answer at a single view between O and N. The argument recorded in D120 must be the review's: FAILED can be set or cleared, and CH2b pruning clears HAVE_DATA, but nTx and the validity bits survive a prune and a pruned block can't be disconnected. The "statuses only rise" line is wrong and is replaced. (2) **`BlockRequestAllowed` (serve.rs:114) must restart the whole decision on the newest view.** The review's invalidate → best-header jump → reconsider interleaving makes the mixed read serve a stale-fork header that Core refuses at every view, and refusing that is the check's whole purpose. The handler mutates nothing before serve.rs:242, so the restart is cheap. Add a seam probe (e.g. `ChainAccess::expired(snap)`) or have that read return `Result`, with a forced-expiry test. (3) CH1 amends `view.rs`'s expiry doc to the two-tier contract: redo the whole decision, or redo a single read where the caller's decision is shown to be single-view-equivalent, as recorded per site in the owner's notes. |

This box's sccache (250G) and ccache (50G) are local disk under /work/cache with no remote backend. **Fit:** our Rust builds use sccache, not ccache. sccache 0.18 has a WebDAV backend ('Ccache/Bazel/Gradle compatible' per its README), but **whether it can read and write bazel-remote's /ac and /cas layout is unverified**; it probably can't. Testing it means contacting the server, so pasta has to say whether to run that test and from where. **Options:** **(i) R730 cache for the runners.** Needs (1) an sccache-compatible endpoint: bazel-remote if the test passes, else a WebDAV service (e.g. nginx-dav or `rclone serve webdav`) beside it in CT 167 under its own hostname on the existing tunnel, with its own size budget (~100–150 GB) apart from Core's 250 GiB; (2) a writer credential per fork as GitHub secrets (`SCCACHE_WEBDAV_USERNAME`/`_PASSWORD` or a token) on PastaPastaPasta/rust-dashcore, used only by push-triggered `ci/*` workflows (reads may stay anonymous). Pros: no monthly cost, uses existing hardware, cache survives GitHub's 7-day eviction. Cons: a write secret sits in a public repo's CI, so a compromised workflow could poison CI build artefacts (CI only, not releases); setup is on pasta's side; and **home uplink egress**. A rough, unmeasured estimate: Rust rlibs are large, a 16-shard tier 1 might pull 30–60 GB per gate, so ~10 GitHub gates/day ≈ 0.3–0.6 TB/day through Cloudflare from home. **(ii) GitHub's cache with the cap raised** (≈ $13/month at 200 GB; needs a payment method). sccache's built-in `gha` backend, no secret, traffic stays inside GitHub's network, 7-day eviction. Simplest and fastest, but it costs money. **(iii) 10 GB default with fewer, bigger shards**: e.g. one dependency-closure cache per toolchain (cargo registry + `target/` deps, built once per integration tip and restored read-only by every shard) and 8 shards instead of 16. Free and no secret, but slower wall time (≈ 40–60 min vs 25–40) and fewer gates in flight. **Recommendation:** start Stage 2's shadow on **(iii)** and measure real per-shard download size and hit rate over its two weeks; it costs nothing and decides nothing irreversible. If more cache is needed, the choice between (i) and (ii) turns on the measured egress: (i) if the uplink can carry it and pasta accepts a write secret in fork CI, otherwise (ii). **Question for pasta:** OK to start on (iii)?
